    • Why it's built this way: the policy is set before the subscriptions so no message a new subscription delivers is refused (AmazonSqsTopologyProvisioner.cs:83-88), and raw delivery keeps the SNS envelope off the body so the receiver sees exactly what the publisher sent. Fail-fast when a required entity is missing follows ADR-070.
    • Where it's used: a singleton built by AmazonSqsTransport.Register (MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Messaging/AmazonSqsTransport.cs:89-94), consumed by AmazonSqsPublisher and AmazonSqsReceiver; covered by MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws.Tests/Messaging/AmazonSqsTopologyTests.cs.

    AmazonSqsTransport

    MMCA.Common.Aws · MMCA.Common.Aws.Messaging · MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Messaging/AmazonSqsTransport.cs:40 · Level 6 · class (internal sealed)

    • What it is: the Amazon SQS/SNS IBrokerTransport, selected by MessageBus:Provider=AmazonSqs (AmazonSqsTransport.cs:43), built on the AWS SDK SQS and SNS clients alone (AmazonSqsTransport.cs:14-16). It registers the connection, provisioner, publisher and receiver that make up the transport.
    • Depends on: IBrokerTransport, BrokerTransportContext, MessageBusSettings, IBrokerPublisher, IBrokerReceivePipeline, AmazonSqsTransportSettings, AwsStaticCredentials, AmazonSqsConnection, AmazonSqsTopologyProvisioner, AmazonSqsPublisher, AmazonSqsReceiver, AmazonSqsDeliveryHandler; AWS SDK RegionEndpoint, FallbackRegionFactory.
    • Concept: a pluggable transport strategy; cross-reference AzureServiceBusTransport for the shared shape. Topology is an SNS topic per event, the host's SQS queue subscribed to each consumed topic with raw delivery, a queue policy limited to those topics, and a {queue}-error queue (AmazonSqsTransport.cs:18-24). [Rubric §10, Messaging & Integration] assesses broker portability and poison-message handling; this transport implements the neutral contract and adapts redelivery to the SQS delay cap. [Rubric §7, Microservices Readiness] also applies: the vendor transport lives only in this adapter package, and the cloud-vendor lists in ArchitectureRules forbid the Amazon namespace root, AWSSDK.* assemblies and MMCA.Common.Aws from cloud-neutral assemblies (MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Testing.Architecture/Rules/Layering/ArchitectureRules.CloudVendors.cs:34,41,55,62).
    • Walkthrough: constants ProviderName = "AmazonSqs" and MaxDelaySeconds = 900 (AmazonSqsTransport.cs:43,46). The public constructor falls back to FallbackRegionFactory.GetRegionEndpoint (AmazonSqsTransport.cs:51-54); an internal one accepts a test fallback (AmazonSqsTransport.cs:58-59). Name returns the provider name and MaxRedeliveryDelay declares 900 seconds (AmazonSqsTransport.cs:62,65). Register (AmazonSqsTransport.cs:71) reads the settings, resolves the region, the static credentials and an absolute ServiceUrl before any service is built, so a configuration error fails while AddBrokerMessaging runs (AmazonSqsTransport.cs:76-84). It then adds TimeProvider.System if absent, the AmazonSqsConnection singleton, the AmazonSqsTopologyProvisioner singleton with settings.ProvisionTopology, the AmazonSqsPublisher as IBrokerPublisher, and the AmazonSqsReceiver hosted service around a new AmazonSqsDeliveryHandler (AmazonSqsTransport.cs:86-109). ReadSettings binds MessageBus:AmazonSqs, else defaults (AmazonSqsTransport.cs:115-121). ResolveRegion(IConfiguration) delegates to the static overload (AmazonSqsTransport.cs:130-131), which uses the configured system name, else the fallback, else throws naming the key and AWS_REGION (AmazonSqsTransport.cs:141-153). LoggerFor<T> falls back to NullLoggerFactory when no logger factory is registered (AmazonSqsTransport.cs:155-156).
    • Why it's built this way: SQS caps a message delay at 15 minutes, so the transport declares that cap as MaxRedeliveryDelay and the framework's receive pipeline shortens every second-level redelivery interval to it (AmazonSqsTransport.cs:27-30). Every header travels packed in one message attribute, so the SQS/SNS 10-attribute cap never applies (AmazonSqsTransport.cs:24-25). All configuration is validated in Register (AmazonSqsTransport.cs:32-37). See ADR-066, ADR-087, ADR-118 and ADR-134.
    • Where it's used: registered by AddAmazonSqsTransport (MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/AwsServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:86-87); MaxDelaySeconds bounds AmazonSqsMessageMapping.ToDelaySeconds (MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Messaging/AmazonSqsMessageMapping.cs:246-247); covered by MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws.Tests/Messaging/AmazonSqsTransportTests.cs.
    • Caveats / not-in-source: the vendor lists also keep entries for the retired MassTransit SQS transport (ArchitectureRules.CloudVendors.cs:39,60), described there as retired vendor transports (ArchitectureRules.CloudVendors.cs:48); this transport itself is built on the AWS SDK clients alone (AmazonSqsTransport.cs:14-16).

    QueueSettlement

    MMCA.Common.Aws · MMCA.Common.Aws.Messaging · MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Messaging/AmazonSqsReceiver.cs:273 · Level 6 · class (private sealed, nested in AmazonSqsReceiver)

    • What it is: the production IAmazonSqsSettlement, bound to one received message's receipt handle and the host's queue and error-queue URLs through a primary constructor (AmazonSqsReceiver.cs:273).
    • Depends on: IAmazonSqsSettlement, AmazonSqsMessageMapping; AWS SDK IAmazonSQS.
    • Concept: the adapter end of the settlement port introduced by IAmazonSqsSettlement; the Azure counterpart is ProcessorSettlement.
    • Walkthrough: DeleteAsync calls DeleteMessage with the receipt handle (AmazonSqsReceiver.cs:275-276). SendToQueueAsync sends ToSendRequest to the queue with the requested delay (AmazonSqsReceiver.cs:278-279). SendToErrorQueueAsync sends it to the error queue with delaySeconds: 0 (AmazonSqsReceiver.cs:281-282). ChangeVisibilityAsync calls ChangeMessageVisibility with the receipt handle (AmazonSqsReceiver.cs:284-285).
    • Why it's built this way: SQS addresses a received message only by its receipt handle, so one small instance per message carries that handle and keeps the handler free of SDK calls.
    • Where it's used: created once per received message in ReceiveLoopAsync (MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Messaging/AmazonSqsReceiver.cs:185); no use outside the defining file.

    RdsIamAuthenticationSettings

    • Concept: cross-reference RdsIamAuthenticationConfigurator, which introduces token-as-password authentication.

    SnsInstallation

    MMCA.Common.Aws · MMCA.Common.Aws.Notifications · MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Notifications/SnsInstallation.cs:12 · Level 0 · record (internal sealed)

    • What it is: the in-memory shape of one row of the DynamoDB installation registry: InstallationId, UserId, Platform, PushChannel and EndpointArn (SnsInstallation.cs:12-17).
    • Depends on: nothing first-party.
    • Concept: cross-reference SnsInstallationRegistry, which explains why SNS needs a side table at all (SNS keeps no owner per endpoint).
    • Walkthrough: a positional record of five non-nullable strings. UserId holds the invariant string form produced by SnsInstallationRegistry.UserKey (MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Notifications/SnsInstallationRegistry.cs:49-50), not the typed identifier. Platform is the normalized value (fcmv1 or apns), which is what lets SnsNativePushSender attribute each endpoint to Android or Apple when it counts an outcome.

    AwsSecretsManagerConfigurationProvider

    MMCA.Common.Aws · MMCA.Common.Aws.Secrets · MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Secrets/AwsSecretsManagerConfigurationProvider.cs:27 · Level 0 · class (internal sealed)

    • What it is: the ConfigurationProvider that reads every AWS Secrets Manager secret under one name prefix into IConfiguration, mapping names the same way the Key Vault provider does so one secret-name convention serves both clouds: the prefix is stripped and -- becomes the configuration separator, so adc/prod/ConnectionStrings--Default arrives as ConnectionStrings:Default (AwsSecretsManagerConfigurationProvider.cs:8-12).
    • Depends on: nothing first-party at runtime; it is built by AwsSecretsManagerConfigurationSource. Externals: Amazon.SecretsManager (IAmazonSecretsManager, ListSecretsRequest, BatchGetSecretValueRequest), Microsoft.Extensions.Configuration (ConfigurationProvider, ConfigurationPath), and BCL PeriodicTimer / CancellationTokenSource (AwsSecretsManagerConfigurationProvider.cs:1-3).
    • Concept introduced, secrets as a configuration source with a fail-loud first load and a fail-soft reload. [Rubric §11, Security] assesses whether secrets live outside code and deployment artifacts and reach the process through an identity-scoped channel: here they are read from Secrets Manager at startup, and the error a missing grant raises names the exact IAM actions to grant (:183-188). [Rubric §29, Resilience & Business Continuity] assesses whether a dependency failure degrades rather than takes down a running system: the first load must succeed (a secret that cannot be read fails startup, naming it, :20-22), but a failed timed reload keeps the values already loaded and records the exception in LastReloadFailure instead of blanking settings the host is using (:124-129). [Rubric §33, Developer Experience] applies through the shared naming convention: a team moving between Azure and AWS keeps one secret-name scheme.
    • Walkthrough:
      • Constants and state. BatchSize = 20 is the most ids one BatchGetSecretValue call accepts (:30); KeyDelimiter is "--" (:32). The client, prefix, optional reload interval, a disposal CancellationTokenSource and the polling task are the fields (:34-38). The constructor null-guards the client and prefix (:44-49).
      • KeyFor(secretName, namePrefix) (:61-69) returns null when the name is outside the prefix (an ordinal StartsWith) or equals the prefix exactly, otherwise the suffix with every -- replaced by ConfigurationPath.KeyDelimiter.
      • Load() (:72-86) blocks once on LoadAsync, under a scoped VSTHRD002 suppression (:78-80). The comment explains why the wait is safe: ConfigurationProvider.Load is synchronous by contract, the AWS SDK v4 client is async only, and no synchronization context is held while the configuration manager adds a source (:74-77). When a reload interval is set and no poller exists yet, it starts PollAsync (:82-85).
      • LoadAsync (:132-143) lists names, then reads them in chunks of BatchSize into a Dictionary keyed with StringComparer.OrdinalIgnoreCase (:135, :137).
      • ListNamesAsync (:145-172) pages ListSecrets with a Name filter on the prefix (:154) and re-checks every name through KeyFor (:161). The re-check matters because the service filter is a case-insensitive prefix match, so a secret for another environment in a different case would otherwise slip in (:13-17).
      • ReadBatchAsync (:174-202) pages BatchGetSecretValue. Any per-secret error throws an InvalidOperationException that lists each secret id, error code and message and states the fix: grant secretsmanager:GetSecretValue (and kms:Decrypt on a customer-managed key) or move the secret out of the prefix (:183-188). Only SecretString values are kept, so a binary-only secret is skipped (:191-196).
      • PollAsync / ReloadOnceAsync (:96-110, :112-130) tick a PeriodicTimer; each tick reloads, clears LastReloadFailure, and only when HasSameData (:204-206) reports a difference swaps Data and calls OnReload(), so an unchanged secret set fires no change token. Any non-cancellation exception is stored, not thrown (:124-129).
      • Dispose() (:89-94) cancels the poller and disposes the token source and the client the provider owns.
    • Why it's built this way: ADR-061 records runtime secret management, and mirroring the Key Vault provider's name mapping and reload posture is what keeps the AWS adapter a drop-in sibling rather than a second convention. Re-checking the prefix ordinally and comparing whole data sets before OnReload keep the provider from importing a wrong-environment secret or churning every IOptionsMonitor on each tick.
    • Where it's used: constructed only by AwsSecretsManagerConfigurationSource.Build (MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Secrets/AwsSecretsManagerConfigurationSource.cs:26-27), which AwsSecretsManagerConfigurationExtensions adds. Covered by MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws.Tests/Secrets/AwsSecretsManagerConfigurationProviderTests.cs.
    • Caveats / not-in-source: no host in MMCA.ADC/Source calls AddAwsSecretsManagerConfiguration, so which deployments read secrets this way is Not determinable from source here.

    S3FileStorageSettings

    MMCA.Common.Aws · MMCA.Common.Aws.Storage · MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Storage/S3FileStorageSettings.cs:13 · Level 0 · class (public sealed)

    • What it is: the options class bound from the FileStorage section for the S3-compatible adapter, one shape that targets AWS S3, Cloudflare R2, MinIO, LocalStack or the GCS XML API (S3FileStorageSettings.cs:13-73).
    • Depends on: nothing first-party. Externals: Amazon.Runtime (RequestChecksumCalculation, ResponseChecksumValidation).
    • Concept introduced, one client, many S3-compatible vendors. [Rubric §32, Dependency & Supply-Chain] assesses whether a vendor dependency is isolated and its defaults are understood rather than inherited: both checksum properties default to WHEN_REQUIRED instead of the AWS SDK v4 default WHEN_SUPPORTED, because the v4 SDK attaches CRC32 trailers and x-amz-checksum-* headers that R2, older MinIO releases and the GCS XML API reject (:58-72). [Rubric §31, Cost & FinOps] is served by the same portability: the bucket vendor is a configuration value.
    • Walkthrough: SectionName is "FileStorage" (:16). Bucket is required and provisioned by infrastructure (:19). Region is the AWS region, or the signing region when ServiceUrl is set (R2 expects auto), and falls back to the SDK chain when blank (:21-26). ServiceUrl selects an S3-compatible endpoint (:28-33); ForcePathStyle switches to {endpoint}/{bucket}/{key} addressing, needed by MinIO and LocalStack (:35-39). PublicBaseUrl overrides the returned URL, for a CDN or R2 public domain (:41-46). AccessKeyId / SecretAccessKey are an optional static pair; setting only one fails registration (:48-56). RequestChecksumCalculation and ResponseChecksumValidation default to WHEN_REQUIRED (:65, :72).
    • Why it's built this way: ADR-134 keeps the core cloud-neutral with vendor adapters beside it, and ADR-045 records the storage contract. Sharing the FileStorage section name with AzureBlobFileStorageSettings means a host switches vendor by its registration call alone.
    • Where it's used: bound by AddS3FileStorage (MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/AwsServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:55-58), which registers nothing when Bucket is blank (:58-61) and resolves the key pair at registration (:64). Read by S3StorageClientFactory, S3ObjectUrl and S3FileStorageService; tested across MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws.Tests/Storage/.

    • What it is: the bound options for SNS mobile push, section NativePush:Sns (SnsNativePushSettings.cs:15), gated by the shared sibling key NativePush:Enabled (SnsNativePushSettings.cs:18).
    • Depends on: DeviceInstallationRequest for the normalized platform constants.
    • Concept: cross-reference AwsServiceCollectionExtensions (AddAmazonSnsPush) for the gate-plus-completeness registration pattern this class feeds.
    • Walkthrough: FcmPlatformApplicationArn and ApnsPlatformApplicationArn (SnsNativePushSettings.cs:21,24) are the SNS platform applications; InstallationsTable (SnsNativePushSettings.cs:27) is the DynamoDB registry; Region, ServiceUrl, AccessKeyId, SecretAccessKey (SnsNativePushSettings.cs:33,36,42,45) follow the package's shared endpoint and credential rules. PlatformApplicationArnFor(platform) (SnsNativePushSettings.cs:58-67) maps DeviceInstallationRequest.FcmV1Platform / ApnsPlatform to the configured ARN, returning null for an unknown platform or a blank ARN.

The completeness check is what turns an incomplete section into a registration no-op rather than a runtime failure (ADR-070).
    • Where it's used: AddAmazonSnsPush (MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/AwsServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:178-190), SnsPushClientFactory, and through IOptions<SnsNativePushSettings> in SnsInstallationRegistry (the table name) and SnsPushDeviceRegistrar (the platform application per platform, MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Notifications/SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:57).

    SnsPushMessage

    MMCA.Common.Aws · MMCA.Common.Aws.Notifications · MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Notifications/SnsPushMessage.cs:12 · Level 1 · class (internal static)

    • What it is: builds the JSON-structured SNS publish body that carries one platform-specific payload per protocol.
    • Depends on: NativePushPayloads for the FCM v1 and APNs payloads; System.Text.Json.
    • Concept: SNS "message structure json": with MessageStructure = "json" (SnsPushMessage.cs:15), SNS reads a dictionary of per-protocol entries and delivers each subscriber its own entry. Reusing the core payload builders keeps the device-side shape identical across the Azure, GCP and AWS push adapters. [Rubric §2, Design Patterns] assesses whether shared logic is reused across adapters; here the vendor adapter only wraps the core payloads.
    • Walkthrough: Build(title, body, metadata) (SnsPushMessage.cs:27) computes the FCM v1 and APNs payloads (SnsPushMessage.cs:29-30) and serializes four entries (SnsPushMessage.cs:32-38): default (the body text), GCM (the FCM payload wrapped in the fcmV1Message envelope), APNS and APNS_SANDBOX (the same APNs payload for both).
    • Why it's built this way: the doc comment records that SNS rejects a JSON-structured publish without a default entry, and that default is what a non-platform subscriber (an operator-added e-mail or SQS subscription) receives (SnsPushMessage.cs:18-21). Duplicating APNs into both keys means one message serves a production or sandbox platform application. See ADR-044.
    • Where it's used: SnsNativePushSender for both targeted and broadcast publishes (MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Notifications/SnsNativePushSender.cs:42,77-78,94).

    AwsSecretsManagerConfigurationSource

    MMCA.Common.Aws · MMCA.Common.Aws.Secrets · MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Secrets/AwsSecretsManagerConfigurationSource.cs:14 · Level 1 · class (internal sealed)

    • What it is: the IConfigurationSource that carries a client factory, a name prefix and an optional reload interval, and builds one AwsSecretsManagerConfigurationProvider from them (AwsSecretsManagerConfigurationSource.cs:14-28).
    • Depends on: AwsSecretsManagerConfigurationProvider; externals IAmazonSecretsManager, IConfigurationSource.
    • Concept: the standard source/provider split of Microsoft.Extensions.Configuration. [Rubric §14, Testability] assesses whether a dependency can be substituted: taking a Func<IAmazonSecretsManager> rather than a client lets a test hand in a fake, and gives each Build a fresh client that the provider owns and disposes.
    • Walkthrough: a primary constructor over clientFactory, namePrefix and reloadInterval (:14-17); NamePrefix (:20) and ReloadInterval (:23) are exposed internally so tests can assert what the extension configured; Build calls the factory and constructs the provider (:26-27).
    • Why it's built this way: the factory defers client construction to the moment configuration is built, after the extension has resolved credentials and endpoint.
    • Where it's used: added by AwsSecretsManagerConfigurationExtensions.AddAwsSecretsManagerConfiguration (MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/AwsSecretsManagerConfigurationExtensions.cs:127-130) only when SecretsManager:NamePrefix is set (:85-94), with a static key pair when one is configured (:97-98), and a ReloadIntervalMinutes that must parse as a positive whole number or the call throws (:103-115).     S3ObjectUrl

    MMCA.Common.Aws · MMCA.Common.Aws.Storage · MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Storage/S3ObjectUrl.cs:9 · Level 1 · class (internal static)

    • What it is: the pure function that composes the absolute URL an S3 upload returns, from the settings, the client's resolved region and the object key (S3ObjectUrl.cs:9-69).
    • Depends on: S3FileStorageSettings; BCL Uri, UriBuilder.
    • Concept: the S3 API returns no object URL on PutObject, so the adapter derives one. [Rubric §14, Testability] assesses whether logic with many branches is isolated where it can be tested without I/O: this is a static function with no client, covered by MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws.Tests/Storage/S3ObjectUrlTests.cs.
    • Walkthrough: Compose (:29-63) escapes the key first (:34), then takes the first branch that applies. A PublicBaseUrl that is absolute wins: {PublicBaseUrl}/{key}, without repeating the bucket (:36-39). An absolute ServiceUrl gives {ServiceUrl}/{bucket}/{key} when ForcePathStyle is set (:45-48), otherwise a UriBuilder prepends the bucket to the host (:50-51). Otherwise AWS S3 in the resolved region, path-style or virtual-hosted (:60-62); with no region known it throws an InvalidOperationException naming the three settings that would fix it (:54-58). EscapeKey splits on /, applies Uri.EscapeDataString to each segment and rejoins, so separators survive and spaces or non-ASCII characters do not break the URL (:65-66). TrimEnd drops a trailing slash from a base (:68).
    • Why it's built this way: the doc orders the branches explicitly (:11-23) because each vendor addresses objects differently; a CDN or R2 public domain already points at the bucket, so repeating it would produce a broken link.
    • Where it's used: S3FileStorageService.UploadAsync composes the URL before uploading (MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Storage/S3FileStorageService.cs:46).

    S3StorageClientFactory

    MMCA.Common.Aws · MMCA.Common.Aws.Storage · MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Storage/S3StorageClientFactory.cs:10 · Level 1 · class (internal static)

    • What it is: maps S3FileStorageSettings onto an AmazonS3Config and creates the IAmazonS3 client, with static credentials or the AWS default chain (S3StorageClientFactory.cs:10-54).
    • Depends on: S3FileStorageSettings, AwsClientEndpoint, AwsStaticCredentials; externals Amazon.S3 (AmazonS3Config, AmazonS3Client).
    • Concept: endpoint and credential selection shared with the other AWS adapters through AwsClientEndpoint and AwsStaticCredentials, so S3, Secrets Manager and SNS resolve regions and keys the same way. [Rubric §11, Security] assesses whether production uses an identity rather than a stored key: with no key pair configured the client falls back to the default credential chain (an IAM role in production).
    • Walkthrough: SectionPath = "FileStorage" names the section in configuration errors (:13). BuildConfig (:23-37) copies ForcePathStyle and both checksum modes (:27-32), then lets AwsClientEndpoint.Apply choose between a service URL with a signing region and a plain region (:34). Create (:45-53) resolves credentials (:48) and constructs AmazonS3Client with or without them (:50-52).
    • Why it's built this way: keeping the mapping in a static factory makes the config testable without a network call (MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws.Tests/Storage/S3StorageClientFactoryTests.cs). ADR-134 and ADR-045 cover the adapter and the storage contract.
    • Where it's used: AddS3FileStorage resolves the key pair against SectionPath at registration, so a half-configured pair fails there rather than on the first upload, then registers Create as the IAmazonS3 singleton with TryAddSingleton, so a client the host registered first wins (MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/AwsServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:65-67).

    RdsIamAuthenticationConfigurator

    MMCA.Common.Aws · MMCA.Common.Aws.Rds · MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Rds/RdsIamAuthenticationConfigurator.cs:20 · Level 2 · class (internal sealed)

    • What it is: an INpgsqlDataSourceConfigurator that replaces the PostgreSQL password with a short-lived RDS IAM token signed locally for each connection string's own host, port and user.
    • Depends on: INpgsqlDataSourceConfigurator, ConnectionStringSettings, DataSourcesSettings, RdsIamAuthenticationSettings; Npgsql NpgsqlDataSourceBuilder.UsePeriodicPasswordProvider, AWS SDK RDSAuthTokenGenerator.
    • Concept introduced, token-as-password database auth. Instead of a stored password, the data source asks a periodic provider for a SigV4-signed token with a 15-minute lifetime (RdsIamAuthenticationConfigurator.cs:12-15). Because the token is a bearer credential sent as the password, the configurator refuses any connection string that does not require TLS. [Rubric §11, Security] assesses credential handling; here no database password exists to leak, and the transport is forced encrypted. [Rubric §7, Microservices Readiness] is also relevant: it plugs into the core data-source configurator, so every context and the readiness check share one data source per connection string (MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/AwsServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:92-94).
    • Walkthrough: primary-constructor parameters region and credentials, both nullable (RdsIamAuthenticationConfigurator.cs:20). TokenRefreshInterval is 10 minutes (RdsIamAuthenticationConfigurator.cs:24) and FailureRetryInterval 30 seconds (RdsIamAuthenticationConfigurator.cs:27). Configure(builder) (RdsIamAuthenticationConfigurator.cs:31) checks TLS on the builder's connection string (RdsIamAuthenticationConfigurator.cs:35-37) and installs UsePeriodicPasswordProvider(GenerateTokenAsync, ...) (RdsIamAuthenticationConfigurator.cs:39). EnsureConfiguredConnectionStringsAreEncrypted(configuration) (RdsIamAuthenticationConfigurator.cs:50) checks the top-level ConnectionStrings:PostgreSQLConnectionString and every DataSources:{name}:PostgreSQLConnectionString already in configuration (RdsIamAuthenticationConfigurator.cs:54-59), parsing each through NpgsqlDataSourceBuilder so it sees what Configure will see (RdsIamAuthenticationConfigurator.cs:65-66). GenerateTokenAsync (RdsIamAuthenticationConfigurator.cs:75) requires a Host and Username (RdsIamAuthenticationConfigurator.cs:82-86), then picks one of four RDSAuthTokenGenerator.GenerateAuthTokenAsync overloads by which of credentials and region are set (RdsIamAuthenticationConfigurator.cs:90-105). EnsureEncrypted (RdsIamAuthenticationConfigurator.cs:108) accepts Require, VerifyCA or VerifyFull (RdsIamAuthenticationConfigurator.cs:110) and otherwise throws a message naming the source and the fix (RdsIamAuthenticationConfigurator.cs:115-118).
    • Why it's built this way: refreshing every 10 minutes keeps the current token well inside its 15-minute lifetime (RdsIamAuthenticationConfigurator.cs:23). Checking configured connection strings at registration makes a cleartext misconfiguration fail at startup, while Configure still catches a connection string supplied later (ADR-070). See ADR-113 and ADR-134.
    • Where it's used: constructed and registered by AddAwsRdsIamAuthentication (MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/AwsServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:125,140); exercised by MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws.Tests/Rds/AwsRdsIamAuthenticationTests.cs.
    • Caveats / not-in-source: SQL Server, SQLite and Cosmos sources are untouched (AwsServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:99-100), since this only implements the Npgsql configurator.

    SnsPushClientFactory

    MMCA.Common.Aws · MMCA.Common.Aws.Notifications · MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Notifications/SnsPushClientFactory.cs:12 · Level 2 · class (internal static)

    • What it is: builds the SNS and DynamoDB clients the SNS push adapter needs, from one SnsNativePushSettings and optional static credentials.

    SnsInstallationRegistry

    MMCA.Common.Aws · MMCA.Common.Aws.Notifications · MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Notifications/SnsInstallationRegistry.cs:20 · Level 2 · class (internal sealed)

    • What it is: the DynamoDB-backed table of push installations (one item per installation id) plus the SNS endpoint-removal helper, used because SNS keeps no owner per endpoint.
    • Depends on: SnsInstallation, SnsNativePushSettings; AWS SDK IAmazonDynamoDB and IAmazonSimpleNotificationService.
    • Concept introduced, ownership rules as conditional writes. The IPushDeviceRegistrar contract says an installation id may be claimed when it is new, already the caller's, or another user's whose current push channel the caller presents (the same device changing hands). Rather than read-then-write, the rule is one DynamoDB ConditionExpression, OwnershipCondition (SnsInstallationRegistry.cs:36), so a race between two writers cannot both win. [Rubric §8, Data Architecture] assesses consistency under concurrency; here the guarantee is atomic at the store. [Rubric §11, Security] applies too: a user cannot hijack another device's installation.
    • Walkthrough: UserIdIndexName = "UserId-index" (SnsInstallationRegistry.cs:29) names the GSI used for user-targeted sends; it must project at least EndpointArn and Platform (SnsInstallationRegistry.cs:26-27), since the sender reads both from each row. UserKey (SnsInstallationRegistry.cs:49-50) is the invariant-culture string form of a user id. GetAsync (SnsInstallationRegistry.cs:56) reads with ConsistentRead = true (SnsInstallationRegistry.cs:63) because ownership decisions follow. TryPutAsync (SnsInstallationRegistry.cs:74) writes the five attributes (SnsInstallationRegistry.cs:76-83) under the ownership condition (SnsInstallationRegistry.cs:92) and returns false on ConditionalCheckFailedException (SnsInstallationRegistry.cs:102-105). QueryByUserAsync (SnsInstallationRegistry.cs:112) pages the GSI until LastEvaluatedKey is empty (SnsInstallationRegistry.cs:140-145). DeleteOwnedAsync (SnsInstallationRegistry.cs:157) and DeleteIfEndpointAsync (SnsInstallationRegistry.cs:168) are conditional deletes through DeleteWhenAsync (SnsInstallationRegistry.cs:178), which swallows a failed condition because the row has changed hands or endpoints (SnsInstallationRegistry.cs:195-198). RemoveEndpointAsync (SnsInstallationRegistry.cs:175-176) is a single DeleteEndpoint call, idempotent on the SNS side. FromItem (SnsInstallationRegistry.cs:204) returns null for a row missing the id, user or endpoint (SnsInstallationRegistry.cs:209-213).
    • Why it's built this way: the conditional deletes make cleanup safe against interleaving: an owner delete leaves a row that was handed over (SnsInstallationRegistry.cs:150-151), and a stale-endpoint cleanup leaves a row refreshed onto a new endpoint (SnsInstallationRegistry.cs:161-162). See ADR-044.
    • Where it's used: registered as a singleton in AddAmazonSnsPush (MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/AwsServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:193); consumed by SnsNativePushSender and SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.
    • Caveats / not-in-source: the table and its UserId-index GSI are provisioned outside the application (AwsServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:161-163); nothing here creates or validates them.

    EndpointTally

    MMCA.Common.Aws · MMCA.Common.Aws.Notifications · MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Notifications/SnsNativePushSender.cs:158 · Level 2 · class (private sealed, nested in SnsNativePushSender)

    • What it is: the per-platform counter one SnsNativePushSender send accumulates and then turns into a NativePushOutcome measured in endpoints (SnsNativePushSender.cs:154-158).
    • Depends on: DeviceInstallationRequest for the fcmv1 / apns constants, NativePushOutcome and NativePushDeliveryUnit.
    • Concept: cross-reference NativePushOutcome for the shared outcome contract; this class is only the SNS adapter's way of filling it in.
    • Walkthrough: four private counters (Android sent and failed, Apple sent and failed) and the first failure seen (SnsNativePushSender.cs:160-164). Delivered sums the two sent counters and feeds the "published to N endpoints" log line (SnsNativePushSender.cs:166). Fail(exception) keeps only the first failure (SnsNativePushSender.cs:168). Count(platform, failure) records any failure, then increments the sent or failed counter for DeviceInstallationRequest.FcmV1Platform (Android) or ApnsPlatform (Apple) by ordinal comparison (SnsNativePushSender.cs:170-199); a platform that is neither is not counted, though its failure still is (SnsNativePushSender.cs:155-156). ToOutcome() calls NativePushOutcome.From with NativePushDeliveryUnit.Endpoint and the five values (SnsNativePushSender.cs:201-202).
    • Why it's built this way: SNS delivers per platform endpoint, not per installation, so the outcome unit is the endpoint, and the platform is read from the endpoint's rows because every row of one endpoint group registered the same token on the same platform (SnsNativePushSender.cs:69-71). A failed user lookup goes through Fail alone, so the send reports a failure without inventing a platform count for devices it never saw (SnsNativePushSender.cs:60-61).
    • Where it's used: only inside SnsNativePushSender.SendToUsersAsync (SnsNativePushSender.cs:47,61,71,75-76); no usage outside the defining file.

    SnsNativePushSender

    MMCA.Common.Aws · MMCA.Common.Aws.Notifications · MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Notifications/SnsNativePushSender.cs:36 · Level 3 · class (internal sealed partial)

    • What it is: the SNS implementation of INativePushSender: a per-user send publishes to each of the users' endpoints and returns a NativePushOutcome counted in endpoints per platform. There is no broadcast path and no topic.
    • Depends on: INativePushSender, SnsInstallationRegistry, SnsInstallation, SnsPushMessage, EndpointTally, NativePushOutcome; AWS SDK SNS client, LoggerMessage source generation. The constructor takes the registry, the SNS client and a logger, no settings (SnsNativePushSender.cs:36-39).
    • Concept: cross-reference SnsInstallationRegistry. [Rubric §29, Resilience & Business Continuity] assesses partial-failure behavior; here a lookup failure skips only that user and a publish failure skips only that endpoint, and both still surface in the returned outcome rather than vanishing into a log.
    • Walkthrough: SendToUsersAsync (SnsNativePushSender.cs:42) builds the message once (SnsNativePushSender.cs:46) and a fresh EndpointTally (SnsNativePushSender.cs:47), de-duplicates user keys (SnsNativePushSender.cs:49), and queries each user's installations; a service or transport failure is logged, recorded with tally.Fail, and the loop continues (SnsNativePushSender.cs:54-63). Rows are grouped by EndpointArn so two installation ids sharing one endpoint get one publish (SnsNativePushSender.cs:65), and each publish result is counted against the group's platform (SnsNativePushSender.cs:67-71). The method logs tally.Delivered and returns tally.ToOutcome() (SnsNativePushSender.cs:75-76). TryPublishAsync (SnsNativePushSender.cs:81) targets the endpoint (SnsNativePushSender.cs:88) and returns null on success (SnsNativePushSender.cs:93); EndpointDisabledException or NotFoundException triggers stale cleanup and is returned as the failure (SnsNativePushSender.cs:95-99), and any other service or transport failure is logged and returned (SnsNativePushSender.cs:100-104). RemoveStaleAsync (SnsNativePushSender.cs:111) removes the endpoint once and then every row pointing at it with the endpoint-conditional delete (SnsNativePushSender.cs:116-120), logging rather than throwing on failure (SnsNativePushSender.cs:122-125). Five source-generated log methods follow (SnsNativePushSender.cs:128-141). IsTransportFailure (SnsNativePushSender.cs:150-152) treats HttpRequestException, IOException, TimeoutException and OperationCanceledException as transport failures only while the caller's token is not cancelled, the same predicate as S3FileStorageService (SnsNativePushSender.cs:143-149).
    • Why it's built this way: the AWS SDK lets connect, DNS, dropped-connection and timeout failures escape unwrapped, so they are handled like a service failure, while the caller's own cancellation always propagates (SnsNativePushSender.cs:143-149). A disabled or missing endpoint counts as failed because its device was not reached; the class doc notes that a bad APNs credential shows up this way (SnsNativePushSender.cs:26-31). Removing every row for a stale endpoint means no surviving row fails its own publish later (SnsNativePushSender.cs:107-110). See ADR-044.
    • Where it's used: registered transient as INativePushSender by AddAmazonSnsPush (MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/AwsServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:194), replacing NullNativePushSender; covered by MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws.Tests/Notifications/SnsNativePushSenderTests.cs.
    • Caveats / not-in-source: how callers act on the returned outcome is outside this file.

    SnsPushDeviceRegistrar

    MMCA.Common.Aws · MMCA.Common.Aws.Notifications · MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Notifications/SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:32 · Level 4 · class (internal sealed)

    • What it is: the SNS implementation of IPushDeviceRegistrar: it maps a device installation onto an SNS platform endpoint, records ownership in the DynamoDB registry, and removes a user's endpoints and rows on request.
    • Depends on: IPushDeviceRegistrar, DeviceInstallationRequest, Result, Error, SnsInstallationRegistry, SnsInstallation, SnsNativePushSettings; AWS SDK SNS client.
    • Concept: cross-reference SnsInstallationRegistry for the ownership rule, and AzureNotificationHubDeviceRegistrar, whose rule this one states it mirrors (SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:163-164). Errors come back as Result failures, not exceptions. [Rubric §29, Resilience & Business Continuity] assesses compensation on partial failure; the registrar orders and undoes SNS side effects so no endpoint is left without a row to account for it.
    • Walkthrough: UpsertAsync (SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:39) normalizes fcmv1 / apns case-insensitively (SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:43-48), returns PushDevice.UnsupportedPlatform validation failures for an unknown platform (SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:49-55) or one without a configured platform application (SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:57-65), and maps a service or transport failure to PushDevice.UpsertFailed (SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:71-77). RegisterAsync (SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:173) reads the row, returns PushDevice.InstallationConflict (SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:156-160) when ClaimedByAnotherDevice is true (SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:168-171,180-183), ensures an endpoint (SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:185), and writes the row with TryPutAsync (SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:187-188). A refused write removes the endpoint only when this request created it (SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:195-198); a row that moved endpoints has its old endpoint removed (SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:203-208). EnsureEndpointAsync (SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:218) reuses a same-platform endpoint via TryRefreshEndpointAsync, which sets Token and Enabled = "true" and returns false on NotFoundException (SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:239-260); otherwise it calls CreatePlatformEndpoint (SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:228-231). DeleteAsync (SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:82) returns success for an unknown or foreign id (SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:88-93), removes the endpoint only when no other row of the same user shares it (SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:99-106), then deletes the row conditionally on the owner (SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:108); failures map to PushDevice.DeleteFailed (SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:111-118). DeleteAllForUserAsync (SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:128) lists the owner's rows through the UserId index (SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:133), removes each distinct endpoint (SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:134-137), then deletes every row conditionally on the owner (SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:139-142); failures also map to PushDevice.DeleteFailed (SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:146-153). RemoveUnaccountedAsync (SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:263) is best-effort and only logs on failure (SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:269-273). Every catch filters on AmazonServiceException or IsTransportFailure (SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:283-285), the same predicate as S3FileStorageService: HttpRequestException, IOException, TimeoutException or OperationCanceledException, and only while the caller's token is not cancelled.
    • Why it's built this way: the conflict check runs before anything is created so the other device's endpoint and row stay untouched, and the conditional write repeats it atomically for a row that appears in between (SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:177-179). Delete runs endpoint first, row last, so a partial failure leaves a row the client's retry can find (SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:95-98); DeleteAllForUserAsync keeps the same order for the same reason, and a row handed over in the meantime survives its owner-conditional delete (SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:122-127). Reporting success for a foreign id avoids an existence oracle (SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:90-91). CreatePlatformEndpoint is idempotent per token, so a retried registration does not multiply endpoints (SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:215-216). See ADR-044.
    • Where it's used: registered transient as IPushDeviceRegistrar by AddAmazonSnsPush (MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/AwsServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:195), replacing NullPushDeviceRegistrar; covered by MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws.Tests/Notifications/SnsPushDeviceRegistrarTests.cs.
    • Caveats / not-in-source: the source names a known edge: when the conditional write is refused and CreatePlatformEndpoint had returned an existing endpoint for a token registered under another installation id, the undo removes that endpoint too; the other row's next send finds it gone and drops it, and its device re-registers on start (SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:192-194).

    S3FileStorageService

    MMCA.Common.Aws · MMCA.Common.Aws.Storage · MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Storage/S3FileStorageService.cs:23 · Level 4 · class (public sealed)

    • What it is: the S3-compatible implementation of IFileStorageService: AWS S3, Cloudflare R2, MinIO and the GCS XML API through one client, against the single bucket in FileStorage:Bucket, with the same contract as AzureBlobFileStorageService (S3FileStorageService.cs:11-23).
    • Depends on: IAmazonS3, IOptions<S3FileStorageSettings> and ILogger<T> as primary-constructor parameters (:23-26); S3FileStorageSettings, S3ObjectUrl, FileUploadOptions, Result / Error.
    • Concept: the same provider-neutral storage contract introduced at AzureBlobFileStorageService. [Rubric §29, Resilience & Business Continuity] assesses whether a write cannot leave inconsistent state behind: the object URL is composed before the upload, so a configuration that cannot name the object fails before any orphan is written (:44-46). [Rubric §32, Dependency & Supply-Chain] applies through the vendor-specific signing choice in SendsUnsignedPayload.
    • Walkthrough
      • _settings unwraps the options with a null guard (:28); IsConfigured is true (:31). The two UploadAsync overloads carry the same RS0026 suppressions as the Azure sibling (:34, :39).
      • UploadAsync(..., options, ct) (:40-95) composes the URL with the client's resolved region (:46), builds a PutObjectRequest with AutoCloseStream = false because the caller owns the stream (:48-57), sets DisablePayloadSigning when SendsUnsignedPayload is true (:59-62), and copies non-empty ContentDisposition and CacheControl headers (:64-72). Success returns the composed URI (:76-77); AmazonServiceException and transport failures both yield FileStorage.UploadFailed (:79-94).
      • DeleteAsync (:98-125) calls DeleteObjectAsync; the comment notes S3 delete is already idempotent (a missing key answers 204), which matches the contract (:102-106). Failures yield FileStorage.DeleteFailed (:109-124).
      • SendsUnsignedPayload (:137-139) is true only for an absolute HTTPS ServiceUrl. The doc gives the reason (:127-134): Cloudflare R2 does not implement streaming SigV4 chunked signing, TLS already protects the body, AWS S3 keeps the SDK default, and plain-HTTP endpoints (MinIO, LocalStack) must keep it because the SDK refuses an unsigned payload over HTTP.
      • IsTransportFailure (:147-149) answers false once the caller's token is cancelled, so the caller's own cancellation always propagates; otherwise HttpRequestException, IOException, TimeoutException and OperationCanceledException count as transport failures (a timeout after the client's own retries surfaces as either of the last two, :141-146). The SNS adapters reuse this predicate; the Azure sibling's is narrower, with no TimeoutException case (MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure/Storage/AzureBlobFileStorageService.cs:107-109).
    • Why it's built this way: ADR-134 keeps the S3 SDK in a vendor assembly so the core stays cloud-neutral; ADR-045 and ADR-123 define the storage contract it honours. One S3-compatible adapter covering several vendors avoids a near-duplicate adapter per bucket provider.
    • Where it's used: registered by AddS3FileStorage (MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/AwsServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:68) when FileStorage:Bucket is set. Tested in MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws.Tests/Storage/S3FileStorageServiceTests.cs and MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws.Tests/AwsServiceCollectionExtensionsTests.cs.
    • Caveats / not-in-source: no host in MMCA.ADC/Source calls AddS3FileStorage, so which deployments use this adapter, and against which vendor, is Not determinable from source here.

    AwsTelemetrySettings

    MMCA.Common.Aws · MMCA.Common.Aws.Telemetry · MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Telemetry/AwsTelemetrySettings.cs:9 · Level 0 · class (internal sealed)

    • What it is: the AwsTelemetry configuration section that drives AwsTelemetryExporterExtensions: the region gate, the CloudWatch Logs destination, optional endpoint overrides and an optional static key pair (AwsTelemetrySettings.cs:9-33).
    • Depends on: nothing first-party; external IConfiguration.
    • Concept: a settings class read key by key rather than bound through the options pattern, because the exporter decides at registration time whether to register anything at all. [Rubric §13, Observability & Operability] assesses whether telemetry export is configured deliberately per deployment: every value here is nullable, and a blank Region keeps the whole exporter off.
    • Walkthrough:
      • SectionPath (:12): the constant "AwsTelemetry".
      • Properties (:15-33): Region (the gate, :15), LogGroup and LogStream (set together, :18, :21), TracesEndpoint and LogsEndpoint (full URLs overriding the regional X-Ray and CloudWatch Logs OTLP endpoints, :24, :27), AccessKeyId and SecretAccessKey (set together, or neither for the default chain, :30, :33). All are string? with init setters.
      • Read (:38-47): builds an instance by indexing IConfiguration once per property through Key.
      • Key (:52): returns "AwsTelemetry:{name}", so error messages and endpoint lookups name the exact key.
    • Why it's built this way: the pairing rules are enforced by the consumer, not here: the exporter throws when only one of LogGroup / LogStream is set (MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/AwsTelemetryExporterExtensions.cs:212-219) and hands the key pair to AwsStaticCredentials (AwsTelemetryExporterExtensions.cs:112-115).
    • Where it's used: read once by AddAwsTelemetryExporter() (AwsTelemetryExporterExtensions.cs:101), with Key reused for endpoint overrides (AwsTelemetryExporterExtensions.cs:194).

    • What it is: a hosted service that logs one Warning at startup when the DataProtection key ring is persisted to Azure Blob Storage without an at-rest Key Vault key (AzureKeyRingAtRestNoticeService.cs:13-32).
    • Depends on: nothing first-party; externals IHostedService, ILogger<T>, [LoggerMessage].
    • Concept: a non-fatal startup notice for an optional hardening step. [Rubric §11, Security] assesses whether security gaps are surfaced rather than silent: the missing at-rest key is not a failure (see AzureDataProtectionExtensions) but it is named in the log on every start.
    • Walkthrough: KeyVaultKeyUriKey is the constant "DataProtection:KeyVaultKeyUri" (:17), which AzureDataProtectionExtensions also reads as its gate-2 key.     • What it is: one factory, Create(string? managedIdentityClientId), that returns the TokenCredential the Azure adapter signs in with (AzureTokenCredentials.cs:20-23).

[Rubric §11, Security] assesses whether services authenticate with identities rather than shared keys: a blank id yields a plain DefaultAzureCredential (system-assigned identity, or whatever AZURE_CLIENT_ID selects), a set id yields one with ManagedIdentityClientId set to the trimmed value (:21-23).

    IAwsTelemetryCredentialSource

    MMCA.Common.Aws · MMCA.Common.Aws.Telemetry · MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Telemetry/IAwsTelemetryCredentialSource.cs:6 · Level 0 · interface (internal)

    • What it is: the credential source the AWS telemetry signing handler asks for AWSCredentials on every export, with an async and a sync member (IAwsTelemetryCredentialSource.cs:15, :25).
    • Depends on: nothing first-party; external AWSCredentials (AWS SDK).
    • Concept: a small internal port that lets the handler be tested with a fixed credential and lets production resolve the SDK default chain lazily. [Rubric §14, Testability] assesses whether external dependencies can be substituted: the two implementations are StaticAwsTelemetryCredentialSource and DefaultChainAwsTelemetryCredentialSource.
    • Walkthrough:
      • GetAsync(CancellationToken) (:15): returns the credentials or null; the contract allows it to throw too, and the handler treats both as "no credentials" and sends nothing (:8-12).
      • Get(CancellationToken) (:25): the synchronous twin for the exporter's synchronous send path; it may block the calling thread (the OTLP exporter calls it on its own background thread) and never waits on a task (:18-22).
    • Where it's used: chosen once in AddAwsTelemetryExporter() (MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/AwsTelemetryExporterExtensions.cs:124-126), then passed to every AwsSigV4ExportHandler (AwsTelemetryExporterExtensions.cs:238, :252) and to AwsTelemetryCredentialWarmup.

    AwsTelemetryCredentialWarmup

    MMCA.Common.Aws · MMCA.Common.Aws.Telemetry · MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Telemetry/AwsTelemetryCredentialWarmup.cs:19 · Level 1 · class (internal sealed partial, IHostedService)

    • What it is: a hosted service that resolves the AWS default credential chain once while the host starts, so the first telemetry export already finds a credential (AwsTelemetryCredentialWarmup.cs:19-50).
    • Depends on: IAwsTelemetryCredentialSource; externals IHostedService, SuppressInstrumentationScope (OpenTelemetry), [LoggerMessage].
    • Concept: a warm-up that is a latency optimization, never a gate. [Rubric §13, Observability & Operability] assesses whether the telemetry pipeline stays out of its own data and degrades visibly: the lookup runs inside SuppressInstrumentationScope.Begin() (:28) so the chain's own HTTP calls (container or instance metadata, STS) are not traced and exported as noise (:26-27), and every failure is a Warning, never a startup failure.
    • Walkthrough:
      • StartAsync (:24-47): awaits credentials.GetAsync (:31); a null result logs LogNoCredential (:32-35). An OperationCanceledException while the host's token is cancelled is swallowed (:37-40); any other exception logs LogWarmupFailed with its type and message (:42-46, with CA1031 suppressed and justified at :41).
      • StopAsync (:50): nothing.
      • Messages: event 3 LogWarmupFailed and event 4 LogNoCredential, both Warning, both stating that each export retries the default chain and sends nothing until one succeeds (:52-62).
    • Why it's built this way: registered only when no static key pair is configured, as a singleton exposed through AddHostedService (MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/AwsTelemetryExporterExtensions.cs:128-136); the comment there states the chain is warmed at host start, never at registration, and that a failure never fails the host (:128-129). The retry on the next export is DefaultChainAwsTelemetryCredentialSource restarting a failed lookup. Recorded in ADR-134.
    • Where it's used: AddAwsTelemetryExporter() only (AwsTelemetryExporterExtensions.cs:132-135). No MMCA.ADC/Source host calls AddAwsTelemetryExporter() today.

    AzureDataProtectionExtensions

    MMCA.Common.Azure · MMCA.Common.Azure · MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure.Startup/AzureDataProtectionExtensions.cs:20 · Level 1 · class (static, extension<TBuilder>)

    • What it is: AddAzureDataProtection(), which persists the ASP.NET Core DataProtection key ring to one Azure Blob and optionally encrypts it at rest with an Azure Key Vault key, so every replica of a scaled-out host shares one key ring (AzureDataProtectionExtensions.cs:26-28).
    • Depends on: HostIntegrationMarker, HostIntegrationNames and AzureKeyRingAtRestNoticeService; externals IHostApplicationBuilder, DefaultAzureCredential, AddDataProtection, and the Azure PersistKeysToAzureBlobStorage / ProtectKeysWithAzureKeyVault extensions.
    • Concept: the configuration-gated host integration with a startup receipt, taught at AwsDataProtectionExtensions. The receipt is registered only after the gate passes (:71), so a URI that appears only in a configuration source added after this call still trips the startup check (:61-63). [Rubric §29, Resilience & Business Continuity] assesses whether scale-out keeps sessions valid: a shared key ring is what lets cookies and antiforgery tokens minted by one replica decrypt on another.
    • Walkthrough:
      • Keys (:32-43): DataProtection:BlobStorageUri (the gate), DataProtection:ApplicationName (defaults to the host application name), DataProtection:KeyVaultKeyUri (optional; outside Development its absence logs one Warning).
      • Gate 1 (:56-67): blank URI returns the builder untouched, with no receipt, so developer machines, test hosts and the Helpdesk seed keep the in-memory default.
      • Receipt (:69-71): a HostIntegrationMarker named AzureDataProtection.
      • Persistence (:73-81): application name from configuration or the environment (:73-74), one DefaultAzureCredential shared by both sinks so they share a token cache (:76-77), then SetApplicationName(...).PersistKeysToAzureBlobStorage(new Uri(blobStorageUri), credential) (:79-81).
      • Gate 2 (:90-99): reads AzureKeyRingAtRestNoticeService.KeyVaultKeyUriKey (:90); when set, ProtectKeysWithAzureKeyVault (:91-94); when blank and the environment is not Development, registers AzureKeyRingAtRestNoticeService as a hosted service (:95-99).
    • Why it's built this way: the comment at :83-89 records the load-bearing decision: gate 2 is deliberately separate from gate 1 because the Key Vault Crypto User role is granted out of band and can lag the deployment; coupling them would turn an optional hardening gap into a total authentication outage, so a missing key is a Warning, not a failure (:97). Identity requirements (Storage Blob Data Contributor, plus Key Vault Crypto User when gate 2 is on) are stated at :47-50. Recorded in ADR-069, ADR-122 and ADR-134; the GCP sibling is GoogleCloudDataProtectionExtensions.
    • Where it's used: MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Identity.Service/Program.cs:106 and MMCA.ADC/Source/Hosts/UI/MMCA.ADC.UI.Web/Program.cs:47. The tripwire message naming this call is at MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aspire/Integrations/HostIntegrationTripwire.cs:111.

    AzureKeyVaultConfigurationExtensions

    MMCA.Common.Azure · MMCA.Common.Azure · MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure.Startup/AzureKeyVaultConfigurationExtensions.cs:23 · Level 1 · class (static, extension<TBuilder>)

    • What it is: AddAzureKeyVaultConfiguration(), which adds an Azure Key Vault configuration source so every vault secret reads through IConfiguration and overrides the sources added before it (AzureKeyVaultConfigurationExtensions.cs:29-31).
    • Depends on: HostIntegrationMarker, HostIntegrationNames; externals AzureKeyVaultConfigurationOptions, AddAzureKeyVault, DefaultAzureCredential.
    • Concept: secrets as a configuration source, the Azure original of the convention AwsSecretsManagerConfigurationProvider and GoogleSecretManagerConfigurationExtensions mirror. [Rubric §11, Security] assesses whether secrets live outside code and reach the process through an identity-scoped channel: authentication is DefaultAzureCredential (managed identity when deployed), needing Key Vault Secrets User on the vault (:52-56).
    • Walkthrough:
      • Keys (:35-42): KeyVault:Uri (the gate) and optional KeyVault:ReloadIntervalMinutes; unset means the vault is read once at startup.
      • Naming (:45-49): a double dash maps onto the configuration separator, so ConnectionStrings--Default arrives as ConnectionStrings:Default.
      • Gate (:82-92): a blank KeyVault:Uri returns the builder with no receipt, so a URI that only appears in a source added after this call still trips the startup check (:84-88).
      • Reload interval (:94-111): parsed with InvariantCulture; a non-positive or non-integer value throws InvalidOperationException (:106-107) rather than silently falling back to "never reload", per the comment at :96-99.
      • Receipt (:113-115): the AzureKeyVaultConfiguration marker, registered only after the reload interval has parsed.
      • Source (:117): builder.Configuration.AddAzureKeyVault(new Uri(vaultUri), new DefaultAzureCredential(), options); an invalid URI surfaces as UriFormatException (documented at :74-76).
    • Why it's built this way: deliberately not part of AddServiceDefaults(), because service defaults run in every host including developer machines and tests (:59-63). Because ConfigurationManager loads each source as it is added, the vault is read synchronously at this call, so it belongs early in Program.cs (:66-70). Recorded in ADR-061 and ADR-134.
    • Where it's used: every ADC host: MMCA.ADC/Source/Hosts/MMCA.ADC.Gateway/Program.cs:75, MMCA.ADC/Source/Hosts/UI/MMCA.ADC.UI.Web/Program.cs:39, MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Identity.Service/Program.cs:91, MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Service/Program.cs:97, MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.Service/Program.cs:70 and MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Notification.Service/Program.cs:70. The tripwire message naming this call is at MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aspire/Integrations/HostIntegrationTripwire.cs:106.

    AzureMonitorExporterExtensions

    MMCA.Common.Azure · MMCA.Common.Azure · MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure.Startup/AzureMonitorExporterExtensions.cs:12 · Level 1 · class (static, extension<TBuilder>)

    • What it is: AddAzureMonitorExporter(), which exports logs, metrics and traces to Azure Monitor through the Azure Monitor OpenTelemetry distro when APPLICATIONINSIGHTS_CONNECTION_STRING is set (AzureMonitorExporterExtensions.cs:24-28).
    • Depends on: HostIntegrationMarker, HostIntegrationNames; externals AddOpenTelemetry().UseAzureMonitor().
    • Concept: an ordering precondition enforced in code. [Rubric §13, Observability & Operability] assesses whether telemetry is exported reliably and filtered deliberately: the outbox-poll and probe filter processors registered by service defaults must run before this exporter's batch processor, otherwise the spans they un-record are exported anyway (:30-33).
    • Walkthrough: ConnectionStringKey is the public constant "APPLICATIONINSIGHTS_CONNECTION_STRING" (:18). The method first scans the service collection for a non-keyed HostIntegrationMarker named ServiceDefaultsTelemetry and throws InvalidOperationException when it is missing (:44-52), regardless of the gate, so a misordered host fails on a developer machine (:37-41). It then returns when the connection string is blank, with no receipt (:54-59), and otherwise registers its own receipt (:61-63) and calls UseAzureMonitor() (:65).
    • Why it's built this way: the gate keeps local runs free of an Azure dependency, and registering the receipt only after the gate means a connection string that appears in a source added later still trips the startup check (:54-55); the receipt lets the startup tripwire fail a host that has the connection string but not the call (:61-62). Both this exporter and the vendor-neutral OTLP exporter can be active at once, each shipping its own copy (:32-33). Recorded in ADR-041 and ADR-134; the GCP sibling is GoogleCloudTelemetryExporterExtensions and the AWS sibling is AwsTelemetryExporterExtensions.
    • Where it's used: every ADC host, directly after service defaults: MMCA.ADC/Source/Hosts/MMCA.ADC.Gateway/Program.cs:82, MMCA.ADC/Source/Hosts/UI/MMCA.ADC.UI.Web/Program.cs:46, MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Identity.Service/Program.cs:98, MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Service/Program.cs:118, MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.Service/Program.cs:75 and MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Notification.Service/Program.cs:85. The tripwire message naming this call is at MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aspire/Integrations/HostIntegrationTripwire.cs:101.

    DefaultChainAwsTelemetryCredentialSource

    MMCA.Common.Aws · MMCA.Common.Aws.Telemetry · MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Telemetry/DefaultChainAwsTelemetryCredentialSource.cs:19 · Level 1 · class (internal sealed)

    • What it is: the IAwsTelemetryCredentialSource used when no static key pair is configured: it resolves the AWS SDK default credential chain lazily, caches the first non-null result, and retries a failed lookup on the next call (DefaultChainAwsTelemetryCredentialSource.cs:19-116).
    • Depends on: IAwsTelemetryCredentialSource; externals DefaultAWSCredentialsIdentityResolver, AWSCredentials, Lock, Volatile.
    • Concept: a single-flight, success-only cache. [Rubric §12, Performance & Scalability] assesses whether hot paths avoid repeated expensive work: once resolved, both members return the cached credential through a Volatile.Read with no lock (:56-60, :80-84); concurrent async callers share one in-flight lookup.
    • Walkthrough:
      • State (:21-31): the two resolver delegates, two locks (_sync for the async lookup, _synchronousResolution for the sync path), the cached _resolved and the in-flight _lookup task.
      • Constructors: the public one wires DefaultAWSCredentialsIdentityResolver.GetCredentials and GetCredentialsAsync with clientConfig: null (:34-39); the internal one takes any two resolvers, for tests (:44-51).
      • GetAsync (:54-75): under _sync, starts a new ResolveAsync() when there is no lookup or the previous one completed without caching a result (a failure, :65-69), then returns lookup.WaitAsync(cancellationToken) (:74), so one caller's cancellation ends only its own wait.
      • Get (:78-104): throws if already cancelled (:86), then takes _synchronousResolution, re-checks the cache because another exporter thread may have resolved it meanwhile (:89-94), calls the sync resolver (:96) and caches a non-null result (:97-100).
      • ResolveAsync (:106-115): awaits the async resolver and caches a non-null result.
    • Why it's built this way: a null or a thrown lookup is never cached, so a host that starts before its IAM role is available recovers on a later export; AwsTelemetryCredentialWarmup pre-warms the same instance at startup.
    • Where it's used: constructed by AddAwsTelemetryExporter() when the static key pair is absent (MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/AwsTelemetryExporterExtensions.cs:124-125).

    MMCA.Common.Aws · MMCA.Common.Aws.Telemetry · MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Telemetry/StaticAwsTelemetryCredentialSource.cs:7 · Level 1 · class (internal sealed, primary constructor)

    • What it is: the IAwsTelemetryCredentialSource for a configured static key pair: both members return the AWSCredentials passed to its constructor (StaticAwsTelemetryCredentialSource.cs:7-15).
